個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

## Document 292: Record-breaking tanker rates pile pressure on already high fuel prices

Source PDF: all report pdf/Record-breaking tanker rates pile pressure on already high fuel prices.pdf

### Page 1

Crude tanker rates have hit all-time highs amid the prolonged
Iran war and Strait of Hormuz disruption. Restructured oil ﬂows
and a rush for capacity to handle oil transport operating under
military protection have driven rates sharply higher, adding
further pressure to fuel prices alongside strong reﬁnery margins
Rico Luman
Record-breaking tanker rates along with operating in war zones and elevated
insurance premiums are all contributing to high fuel prices

Tanker shipping thrives in risky waters amid
a continued capacity squeeze
Oil tanker markets sit at the centre of geopolitical turmoil, and this
year that has translated into record-breaking freight rates as the
conﬂict in the Middle East drags on. Tankers have been caught up in
sanctions regimes and repeatedly targeted in key shipping lanes,
including the Strait of Hormuz, Bab al-Mandeb and the Gulf of Aden.
At the same time, demand for tanker capacity has surged as

traders seek alternative routes and sources of supply, including

Average global crude vessel earnings exceeded an unprecedented
level of $500,000 per day, 10 times the 2025 average, in early
October. For Suezmax tankers and very large crude carriers (VLCCs,
with a maximum capacity of 2m barrels) alone, rates surged even
beyond that. Crude shipments from Ras Tanura in Saudi Arabia to
Rotterdam made up about $2/bbl in 2025; in September, this

surged beyond $35. Combined with high reﬁnery margins (the
current crack spread of diesel in Europe is about 2.5 times the 2025
average), this could add over $0.50 to the base price of a litre of
diesel at the gas station.
Earnings of product tankers have also tripled compared to the 2025
average. Nevertheless, these have responded much more
moderately. This is mainly because of the key importance of the
Middle East region for crude oil. And when it comes to the

supertankers, the VLCCs, these are designated to transport crude
and traditionally are intensively deployed on the Middle East trade
routes.

### Page 4

Source: Clarksons, ING Research
Crude tanker orders pick up after a
prolonged downturn
Tanker markets are cyclical by nature, and there’s a clear
connection with freight rates. Over the pandemic years of 2020-21,
tanker earnings hit lows due to lower oil consumption and
shipments. This resulted in an order book of just 4% of global crude
carrier capacity, the lowest on record since the 1990s.
With the recovery of oil demand and tanker rates in 2022 and

2023, order intake began to rebound. This year, in particular, order
books have doubled from 14% to 28%. Although the average age
of the crude ﬂeet has also reached a multi-decade high of over 13
years and more scrapping can be expected, we can also expect
more supply pressure. But many of these vessels will only be
delivered in 2028-29, likely after the current boom ends. It’s all
about geopolitics and how the Middle East conﬂict evolves, but
tanker markets could still see a solid 2027.

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.
In the spotlight for October 2026: US-Iran war and EU-China relations

EU-China trade relations

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.
Key events, issues and themes to watch in October 2026

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

The foreign ministers’ meeting comes ahead of the December Leaders’ Summit in Miami under the US’

and technological innovation, a much more transactional focus than previous foreign ministerials, which

typically focused on geopolitical crisis management.
It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

## Document 334: US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

semiconductor share prices have recently lagged the trajectory of near-term EPS
estimates. Similarly, the market trades at a P/E discount relative to what a macro model
would imply given the current level of corporate profitability. Our 12-month S&P 500
return forecast of +14% (to 8,700) reflects the view that earnings growth, rather than
expanding valuations, will remain the primary driver of the bull market.
17 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

17 September 2026   3
Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

### Page 6

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 earnings growth in the next few years will be highly sensitive to the 
trajectory of semiconductor margins. In a scenario where slowing AI infrastructure 
investment, increasing supply, and/or technological shift lowers semiconductor prices 
and profit margins, S&P 500 EPS growth would also disappoint. We estimate that each 
percentage point change in S&P 500 semiconductor gross margins next year would shift 
S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.

### Page 10

“Other income” from private investment gains 
Appreciating equity investment stakes are also temporarily inflating S&P 500 
earnings. Mega-cap tech earnings have recently been boosted by large GAAP “other 
income” related to their equity investments. In Q2 2026, the mega-cap tech companies 
enjoyed unrealized investment gains in private companies that totaled roughly $150 
billion, translating into 12% of S&P 500 EPS. We expect H2 2026 reports will include 
additional “other income” that should be larger than the gains in Q1 but smaller than Q2 
totals. We expect a much smaller contribution in 2027. The complete removal of this 
“other income” next year would create a drag of 8 pp on S&P 500 earnings growth in 
2027 relative to 2026, all else equal.

### Page 11

S&P 500 earnings outlook

Our baseline forecast is for S&P 500 EPS to rise to $415 in 2027 (+11% year/year) 
and to $460 in 2028 (+11%). We expect an above-consensus base of earnings in 2026 
due to further “other income” gains from private equity stakes in H2 of this year. 
Excluding the “other income” inflating earnings in 2026, S&P 500 earnings growth next 
year would register 18%. Our 2027 EPS forecast is similar to the bottom-up consensus 
estimate of $419 but above the median top-down strategist estimate of $403.

### Page 12

Our 2028 EPS forecast is about 5% below the bottom-up consensus, signaling a 
return to the typical historical pattern of downward revisions to consensus earnings 
estimates after the last two years of rising estimates. Outside of the complex of AI 
capex beneficiaries, we expect sales growth will register close to nominal GDP , margins 
will expand modestly, and a growing boost from AI productivity will result in earnings 
growth of 9% in both 2027 and 2028.  Our forecasts for EPS growth among this group of 
companies are roughly 4-5% below consensus estimates in both 2027 and 2028, similar 
to the historical average magnitude of downward revision to consensus estimates. While 
AI investment spending has had a direct impact on the earnings of semiconductor and 
other AI infrastructure stocks, it has also lifted earnings for companies outside of the “ AI 
complex,” including by boosting capital markets activity as well as supporting consumer 
spending via the wealth effect. 
Solid GDP growth and easing pressure from energy prices will be the main macro 
tailwinds for earnings growth in 2027. Economic growth is the primary variable in our 
top-down earnings model and explains more than half of the historical variation in S&P 
500 EPS growth. Our economists expect US real GDP growth will average 2.1% in 2027 
and 2.3% in 2028. Our commodity strategists expect Brent crude oil will decline to 
$80/bbl by the end of 2027, which would weigh modestly on Energy sector profits but 
boost earnings for most other sectors. Outside of the AI-related risks outlined above, 
persistent input cost pressures and risks to the consumer from equity market volatility 
are two of the key macro downside risks to our earnings forecast.

### Page 13

As the earnings tailwind from AI investment spending fades, the trajectory of S&P 
500 profitability in coming years will depend increasingly on the realization of AI 
productivity gains. Our forecasts embed a 1 pp boost to S&P 500 EPS growth from AI 
productivity in 2027 and a 2 pp boost in 2028. Economy-wide surveys and corporate 
commentary signal enterprise adoption that is increasing but remains in early stages, 
and the impact of AI adoption on corporate earnings still appears narrow. However, the 
recent acceleration in enterprise AI spending suggests that the earnings impact of AI 
adoption should become clearer in coming quarters.

### Page 14

Valuing S&P 500 earnings

Like our forecasts and consensus estimates, market pricing suggests investors 
anticipate a deceleration in earnings growth going forward. This is true both for the 
broad market and for AI infrastructure stocks. For example, the share prices of 
semiconductor stocks have historically mirrored the path of consensus EPS estimates, 
and the recent moderation of semiconductor returns has aligned with consensus 
estimates for decelerating growth in 2027 and 2028.

The current S&P 500 P/E multiple signals an optimistic outlook but market 
skepticism regarding the sustainability of current profits. In addition to rising interest 
rates, an anticipated deceleration in earnings helps explain the recent decline in the S&P 
500 forward P/E multiple. Along with macro factors like interest rates, the level of 
corporate profitability has helped explain the variation in S&P 500 valuation over time. 
Based on the historical relationship between S&P 500 ROE and P/E, the current S&P 500 
ROE of 24% — a record high — would point to a forward 12-month P/E of over 21x 
against today’s macro backdrop. Given current inflation and interest rates, today’s S&P 
500 multiple of 19x would be consistent with an ROE of roughly 22%, more than 200 bp 
below the current level but otherwise matching 2021 as the highest on record.

### Page 15

Earnings, rather than valuations, should continue to drive equity market upside 
from here. Our 3-, 6-, and 12-month S&P 500 return targets are +5 (8000), +9% (8300), 
and +14% (8700). These imply a P/E multiple on consensus forward EPS that remains 
close to the current level of 19x. Our economists and interest rate strategists expect 
interest rates to decline modestly in coming quarters, creating some macro upside risk to 
equity valuations. However, we expect investor uncertainty regarding the impact of AI 
on the outlook for long-term profits to persist in coming quarters, making a dramatic 
increase in valuations unlikely.

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
There is an argument—made in the recent Geneva
Report and by various former IMF officials—that
China’s current export surge is the simple
consequence of the housing downturn and the
resulting weakness in Chinese internal demand.*
The weakness in the RMB in this story is entirely
incidental to the increase in exports, which is, more or
less, a mechanical response to weak internal demand.

Cannot sell excavators at home? Export them.
Built more modern car capacity than your internal
market supports? Export that too…
The fall in the nominal RMB over the course of 2022
and the first half of 2023 in nominal and read terms
was simply a function of domestic macroeconomic
weakness, and a growing rate differential. It wasn’t an
active policy choice by China’s central bank.

### Page 3

3
Now, some might note that China’s currency doesn’t
always move with rate differentials. The RMB didn’t
consistently get pulled up when Chinese rates were
higher than U.S. rates (2002-2020 generally
speaking), and while it did fall when rate differentials
moved against the RMB in 2022 and 2023, it then
stabilized and has even appreciated modestly since
the middle of last year. That appreciation hasn’t been
big enough to correct the deep undervaluation of the

RMB, but it came at a time when the PBOC policy
rate (the reverse repo rate) was well below the U.S.
policy rate.
China doesn’t have an open financial account. As a
result, equilibrium in the foreign exchange market is
thus driven far more by exporter conversion and
current account flows than by rate differentials and
financial flows.** That, together with China’s ongoing
management of the exchange rate and a trade surplus
that can generate significant inflows, means that the

### Page 4

4
value of the RMB is much more of a policy variable
under the independent control of the Chinese
authorities than a simple byproduct of monetary and
fiscal policies.
The modern IMF is reluctant to accept this
conclusion. Its surveillance has generally ignored
China’s currency management (even if that
management is relatively obvious; see the data on FX
settlement) and its recent comprehensive assessment
of China’s economy completely ignored the balance of

payments and exchange rate management.
But it is pretty obvious to the market, even if the
PBOC, rather absurdly, insists that “market forces
play a decisive role in determining the RMB
exchange rate.”
I doubt the PBOC can win this argument. I agree
with Mark Sobel:
“The renminbi is still heavily managed and the
authorities have acted to slow appreciation. The paper
states that the PBoC phased out ‘regular’ foreign
exchange intervention in 2017. But it has many

means at its disposal to achieve the same effect
through extensive capital controls, administrative
guidance, signalling at the fix and deploying state-
owned commercial banks. These means are heavily
used. Thus, the word ‘regular’ must be taken with a
pinch of salt. The comparison to Japan, which

### Page 5

5
intervenes occasionally, is spurious – the yen largely
floats freely in integrated global markets. The same
cannot be said for the renminbi.”
In reality, the PBOC’s daily fix now plays the decisive
role in the currency’s path, and the market is often a
bet on how the PBOC’s management of the yuan will
evolve.
That is clear from the lack of volatility in the yuan’s
rate against the dollar. The yuan’s move down against
the dollar in 2022 was a controlled 10 percent move,

and the appreciation over the last 16 months has been
even more controlled crawl.
And the market is clearly massaged with substantial
help from the state banks. In fact that the pattern of
changes in the monthly FX settlement data looks
exactly like the pattern of intervention of a central
bank managing its currency around a tight band (the

### Page 6

6
state banks in practice buy FX to keep the CNY from
testing the strong side of the band, so there is an
asymmetry in how the CNY is managed)
None of this would matter much if China’s economy
was small. But that obviously isn’t the case. Nor would
this matter much if China’s exports didn’t respond to
changes in the RMB’s real (and nominal) value. But
that is also not the case.
There is abundant evidence that China’s exports do
respond to moves in the currency. Periods of RMB

weakness have consistently been associated with
periods of especially rapid Chinese export growth.

### Page 7

7
Chancellor Merz and President Macron got this
right, even if macroeconomic issues haven’t yet
figured prominently in the EU’s dialogue with
China.*** As they note in their open letter to the
European Commission:
“An appreciation of undervalued currencies towards a value
more in line with fundamentals would help support the
rebalancing of trade accounts with the EU in the short and
medium term … European policy discussions focused on
forging a common understanding of these exchange rate issues

and their interaction with global imbalances would be useful.”
The best evidence that exports responded to changes
in the currency, and not simply to changes in the path
of China’s internal demand, is the acceleration in
China’s export growth after the RMB’s depreciation
over the course of 2022.
This is the killer chart.

### Page 8

8
Now, you might ask: what of the acceleration in
export growth in 2020 and 2021? That was pretty
clearly tied to the global increase in goods demand
that came with the pandemic and work from home
requirements. It was expected to fade when the world
economy normalized. But it didn’t. By 2023 Chinese
export growth again topped global trade growth.
The general expectation that the rise in China’s goods
surplus during Covid would prove to be “transitory”

### Page 9

9
heavily influenced the IMF’s work at the time: see the
IMF’s external sector reports of 2023 and 2024.
I would argue that neglecting the exchange rate was
the key reason the IMF’s kept forecasting, incorrectly,
that China’s current account surplus would fall (See
this blog with Shahin Vallee). The IMF certainly
didn’t warn that China’s property downturn and the
RMB’s real depreciation risked a second China shock
in its 2022, 2023, or 2024 surveillance reports.

Concretely, the weaker RMB appears to have
contributed to a very large outperformance of China’s
exports relative to global trade. See the following
chart
That incidentally also happened after the RMB
followed the dollar down in 2003.
In both periods, there were significant structural
shifts. The WTO accession in 2002, and the success

### Page 10

10
of Chinese industrial policies in the “green” industrial
sectors and autos in 2022. In both cses exports were
turbocharged when structural shifts that should have
resulted in a stronger currency were instead amplified
by currency weakness.
No matter. Paul Krugman has noted that a country
that is converging technologically with its trade
partners should see a trend appreciation in its
currency. China got the technological convergence,
but not the real appreciation—at least not until this

year. And the move since the middle of 2025 has
corrected the depreciation of the RMB that occurred
when the RMB followed the dollar down on
liberation day, but not the structural overvaluation.
Now, there is an argument that exchange rate
adjustment is too hot an issue to make it a priority.
The PBOC isn’t interested it seems.
And some believe that in the interest of comity and
good relations with China, the IMF should mirror
the PBOC’s views. After all a stronger RMB would

slow China’s exports and thus slow China’s economy
at a time when China needs export growth to escape
deflation and generate employment. Or so the
argument goes. Concerns about “external balance“
and the negative global spillovers from China’s rising
goods surplus should be ignored and the Fund’s sole
focus should be on policies that lead to internal

### Page 11

11
balance. That provides one rationale for the Fund’s
ongoing decision to essentially ignore the contentious
issue of the PBOC’s ongoing exchange rate
management.
That argument, however, assumes that China’s
economy is small and thus China’s external surplus
can safely be ignored because it isn’t a drag on the
growth of the IMF’s other members.
That clearly isn’t true.
And it isn’t clear that China is more interested in
having a negotiation over its level of basic pension

benefits or the size of its fiscal deficit (where the
IMF continues to make timid recommendations that
would not move the needle—and certainly wouldn’t
meaningfully reduce China’s external surplus) than a
discussion over its currency practices.****
Back in the world of fixed but adjustable exchange
rates, there was an expectation (not always met to be
sure) that surplus countries would allow their
currencies to strengthen to bring their economies

back into external balance, and that other policy tools
would be used to ensure internal balance.
I would argue that China’s exchange rate (against
both the dollar and the basket) has many similarities
to the old world of fixed but adjustable currencies.
True, it moves on a controlled path when it moves

### Page 12

12
between different levels rather than jumping up and
down. But the PBOC does frequently target a level,
and that targeted level does change over time.
I would further argue that steps like the recent tariff
rate quota that will limit China’s exports of hybrid
cars to Europe to around 350,000 units next year (up
from 2025, but well below the 800,000 units of
2026) aren’t going to address the underlying
imbalance on their own. They can provide a bit of a

buffer for specific sectors.
But that buffer, while necessary, won’t address the
broad-based erosion of manufacturing capacity
outside China. This erosion will persist as long as an
undervalued RMB sends the entire world a signal to
use China as its production base.
The new system of managed trade that is emerging in
autos would need to be progressively expanded to an
ever-increasing set of sectors if protection is used as a
substitute for both domestic reform in China and

exchange rate adjustment.
That is now the direction of travel, but it doesn’t
address the underlying systemic issues.
Incidentally, those who believe the path of internal
demand is critical to rebalancing and that exchange
rate moves are secondary need to explain how an
acceleration in domestic demand would, on its own,

### Page 13

13
lead China’s external accounts to adjust. One
important, stylized fact about China is that domestic
demand growth is no longer feeding over to import
growth (ex-gold, that is).
Bottom line: China, which needs a 1.5 percentage
point contribution from net exports every year, is on a
collision course with its trading partners without a
currency correction that provides the basis for more
balanced trade even in the absence of trade
restrictions.
And there isn’t a realistic path towards balance that

doesn’t involve a stronger RMB; there aren’t, as Paul
Krugman once put it, immaculate transfers.
* That downturn shows up in domestic deflationary
pressure, and weak job growth/high rates of (at least
youth) unemployment. China, in other words, isn’t in
“internal balance.” I really liked the report from

### Page 14

14
Kamakshya Trivedi and Hui Shan of Goldman Sachs
arguing China needs two tools to hit two targets
(internal and external balance).
** To be sure, rate differentials matter for exporter
conversion—but so does the expected movement of
the RMB. The market understands this better than
the Fund. The Chen and Suwanapruti (Goldman
Sachs) note uses a framework similar to mine to
describe the CNY’s moves in Q3:
“In our view, sizeable FX inflows have continued to support

RMB appreciation despite unfavorable interest rate
differentials, as strong export receipts and increased exporter
FX conversions have outweighed portfolio outflows. Our
estimated FX conversion ratio rose to 63% in August,
compared with around 45% in normal years, likely reflecting a
shift in market expectations from RMB depreciation to
appreciation.”
*** Š ef č ovi č  has jurisdiction over trade policy
(narrowly defined), but not broader macroeconomic

policies. Europe’s institutional architecture for
exchange rate coordination is not well-developed.
**** The current account surplus is no longer masked
to the same extent as in 2023 and 2024, as SAFE has
fiddled with its BoP methodology once again (per the
Federal Reserve) and the BoP goods surplus is no
longer significantly below the customs goods surplus.
Of course, there are still problems in the data—the
deficit in the investment income account most

### Page 3

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月
◆現状：100ドルを挟んで乱高下
８月のWTI原油先物価格は 、中旬に80ドル台
後半に上昇。米国とイランの交渉が難航したほか 、
イエメンの武装組織フーシがタンカー等を攻撃したこと
で、石油供給の減少が意識されたことが背景。下旬
には、80ドル台前半に下落 。ウォーシュ議長のタカ
派的な発言を受けて、FRBの利上げ観測が強まり、
米国需要が伸び悩むとの見方が広がったことが主因 。
８月末から９月中旬にかけて 、原油価格は100
ドル台に急騰。米国とイランの間で攻撃の応酬が続
いたことが背景。加えて、フーシがバブ・エル・マンデブ
海峡付近の港湾都市を掌握したことや 、サウジアラ
ビアの東西パイプラインが攻撃を受けて操業を停止し
たことで、ホルムズ海峡を迂回するルートからの石油
輸出が停滞するとの懸念も価格を押し上げ。下旬に
は、東西パイプラインの輸送能力が早期に回復する
との報道を材料に、原油価格は90ドル台に下落。
◆投機筋のネットポジションは小幅に拡大

投機筋の買い越し幅は拡大。中東情勢の緊迫化
を受けて、原油価格の上昇を見込む投資家が増加
している可能性。
◆見通し：当面100ドル前後で高止まり
両国の戦闘がこれ以上激化しないことを前提とす
るメインシナリオでは、原油価格が100ドルを中心と
する高値圏で推移する 見通し。ホルムズ海峡の封
鎖は続く一方、東西パイプラインなどの代替ルートや 、
中東産油国の生産が徐々に回復することが背景。

### Page 4

原油
(株)日本総合研究所 原油市場展望 2026年９月3
◆米国のガソリン・ディーゼル価格が高騰
米国では 、燃料油の価格が高騰 。足元のレギュ
ラーガソリン小売価格は全米平均で 4.5ドル/ガロン
と、消費者の心理的節目とされる４ドルを超える水
準。トラックや農業機械の燃料となるディーゼルの価
格は、統計開始以来の最高値に上昇。
◆製油業者のマージン確保や増税も影響
こうした石油製品価格が高騰する一因は 、原料と
なる原油の価格上昇。ただし、次の２点が、過去の
原油高局面よりも製品価格を高める方向に作用。
第１に、製油業者による強気のマージン設定 。世
界的な石油製品の需給ひっ迫が主因 。ホルムズ海
峡の封鎖を受けて 、中東からの製品輸出が減少し
たほか、主に中東産原油を精製していたアジアからの
輸出も減少。ウクライナによる石油関連インフラへの
攻撃を背景に、ロシアからの輸出も下振れ。
第２に、州政府による増税。連邦政府は、1993
年の増税以降、燃料税を据え置いている一方、多く
の州は、環境対策などの観点から増税を実施 。直

近の原油高局面（2022年）に比べて、州政府に
よる増税分が小売価格を押し上げる構図に。
◆燃料高は、米中間選挙で共和党の逆風に
燃料価格の高騰は、11月に実施される米中間選
挙において、現在の与党である共和党の逆風となる
可能性。ガソリン高が無党派層の不満を強めるほか、
ディーゼル高がトラック運転手や農家などの共和党の
伝統的な支持基盤の反発を招く可能性も。
トピック：米国のガソリン・ディーゼル価格が高騰する背景

## Document 482: 国内9月社融数据解读：结构仍以财政发力为主

韧性 vs 偏弱
①总量：2026年9月社会融资规模增量 3.55万亿元，同比多增约153亿元；存量468.17万亿元，
同比增速 7.1%，较上月回落 0.1 个百分点。
②结构：主要拉动项为 政府债券（约2.00万亿，同比多增约0.5万亿）；主要拖累项为 人民币贷
款（1.27万亿，同比少增约0.33万亿）。
③货币供应：M2 同比 7.2%，M1 同比 3.2%，M2-M1 剪刀差走阔至4.0pct。

④需求侧：政府债净融资发力支撑总量；企业中长期贷款 8900 亿元保持韧性，居民中长期贷款
874 亿元小幅回升，私人部门信用仍偏弱。
⑤一句话定性：本月数据整体符合预期，核心矛盾仍为财政靠前发力 vs 私人部门内生需求偏弱。
浙 商 期 货 宏 观 研 究 团 队
浙商期货有限公司期货交易咨询业务资格：证监许可[2011]1287号

余额环比增量（亿元）。M2 余额较8月+约2.82万亿元，M1 余额较8月+约0.98万亿元。

### Page 3

M2 分析：M2 同比回落至 7.2%，连续回落，反映信贷投放对货币创造的拉动减弱、财政存款投
放对冲力度有限，广义货币增速中枢下移。
M1 分析：M1 同比降至 3.2%，显示企业活期存款增速放缓，企业投资与扩产意愿偏谨慎，经营
活跃度边际走弱。
M2-M1 剪刀差：剪刀差走阔至 4.0pct，资金淤积于定期存款、活化不足，经济内生动能偏弱。
注：2024年起M1统计口径调整，纵向比较需关注口径变化。

为增速变动（百分点），非增量；④人民币贷款为社融口径下的分项，与3.2节"金融机构新增人民币贷款"口径略有差
异。

### Page 6

2026年9月社融增量3.55万亿元，同比多增。主要支撑来自政府债券前置发力，政府债净融资
约2.00万亿元，同比多增约0.50万亿元，占社融比重约56%，为年内高位水平。拖累项为人
民币贷款，同比少增约0.33万亿元。存量增速7.1%，仍高于名义GDP增速，金融对实体的支
持力度总体稳固，但边际有所减弱。
3.2 金融机构新增人民币贷款
分项 2026-09（亿元） 同比多增/少增（亿元）

居民短期 -900 -2321
居民中长期 874 -1626
企业短期 4600 -2500
企业中长期 8900 -200
票据融资 -500 +3526
非银金融机构 -1485 —
注：①9月金融机构新增人民币贷款 1.08万亿元，同比少增约 0.21万亿元；②分项中居民短期-900亿、居民中长期874
亿（地产销售端拉动边际企稳但仍偏弱）、企业短期4600亿、企业中长期8900亿（基建/制造业融资韧性）、票据-500
亿（银行冲量压力缓解）、非银-1485亿；③"同比多增/少增"为较上年同期的增减（亿元），负值表示少增。

2026年9月金融机构新增人民币贷款1.08万亿元，同比少增约0.21万亿元。结构特征：企业中
长期贷款8900亿元保持较高水平，显示基建/制造业融资需求相对稳定；票据融资-500亿元，
反映银行以票冲贷压力明显缓解、信贷投放节奏相对均衡。居民中长期贷款874亿元，地产销
售端对信贷的拉动边际企稳但仍偏弱。

### Page 7

四、综合结论
4.1 信号矩阵定位
货币供应 货币需求 组合含义
宽（M2 7.2%，回落中） 弱（信贷增长乏力） 流动性淤积、资产荒，需财政发力
4.2 总体结论
总量：9月社融增量3.55万亿，存量增速7.1%，M2同比7.2%，社融-M2缺口-0.1pct，反映
财政发力对总量形成支撑，但信用扩张动能偏弱。
结构：政府债同比多增约0.50万亿是核心亮点，企业中长期贷款8900亿维持韧性，居民中长
期贷款小幅回升但仍偏弱，私人部门信用扩张缓慢。
核心矛盾：财政靠前发力 vs 私人部门不接棒。M1同比3.2%、剪刀差走阔至4.0pct，显示企
业资金活化程度偏低、经济内生动能不足。
政策含义：货币政策需维持宽松（降准降息仍有空间），财政需持续加快支出进度并保持专项
债、超长期特别国债发行节奏。
前瞻：下月关注政府债发行节奏、地产销售能否企稳、企业中长期贷款能否延续回升。
